```
SOMNIUM — PROJECT STATE


Last updated: 2026-11-04

Project

Babylon.js procedural open-world simulation with experimental three-dimensional Poincaré geometry.

Existing Systems
Heightmap-based terrain with streamed chunks and LOD.
Procedural ecology, vegetation and geology.
Regional water, hydrology, rainfall and clouds.
Adaptive graphics management.
SpatialTransform and GeometryRenderer.
Experimental GPU geometry shader system.
Hyperbolic camera navigation.
Gameplay
Flight-simulator perspective.
Third-person character perspective.
Main menu with perspective selection.
Runtime perspective switching.
Character movement and terrain following.
Third-person camera with scroll-wheel zoom.
Celestial System — New Milestone

New module: src/environment/celestial-system.js

Implemented:

Visible sun and moon.
Orbital day/night cycle.
Animated directional sunlight.
Animated moonlight.
Variable ambient illumination.
Dynamic sky background coloration.
Configurable orbital speed and day length.
Celestial diagnostics.

Terrain lighting:

src/world/world-manager.js
Terrain material now receives Babylon.js lighting instead of explicitly disabling it.
Existing vertex colors are preserved.
Small emissive contribution maintains nighttime visibility.

Initial day length: 900 seconds.

Coordinate Architecture
Authoritative simulation coordinates remain separate from rendering coordinates.
Poincaré projection handles spatial distortion.
Celestial bodies use camera-relative visual positioning.
Celestial orbital direction and lighting are shared between gameplay perspectives.
Immediate Priorities
Validate terrain illumination during daylight and nighttime.
Verify sun and moon visibility from both camera perspectives.
Tune ambient illumination and terrain coloration.
Introduce atmospheric scattering and dawn/dusk coloration.
Integrate moving shadows.
Introduce camera orbit controls.
Continue hyperbolic character movement and geodesic navigation.
Architectural Requirements
Preserve existing world and environmental systems.
Keep simulation independent of rendering quality.
Maintain one persistent world across camera modes.
Never allow navigation to reach the Poincaré boundary.
Keep celestial lighting independent of camera perspective.
Validate deployed changes before marking them complete.
Source

Source tree filepath description:
https://theinfinitycorporation.com/somniumsunrise/toomdeveloamforlange/source.txt

Future sessions should inspect the latest deployed code before modifying existing modules. Proposed implementations must not be confused with verified deployments.


## Celestial System — Atmospheric Rendering Milestone

Primary module:
src/environment/celestial-system.js

Scene integration:
src/scene/create-scene.js

### Existing Celestial Simulation

- Visible sun and moon.
- Orbital day/night cycle.
- Configurable day length.
- Animated directional sunlight.
- Animated moonlight.
- Variable ambient illumination.
- Shared celestial directions across gameplay perspectives.
- Time-of-day controls.
- Celestial diagnostics.

### Atmospheric Rendering

New procedural atmospheric sky renderer.

Implemented in the proposed CelestialSystem revision:

- GPU-based atmospheric sky shader.
- Camera-centered sky dome.
- Blue daytime sky with zenith-to-horizon gradient.
- Dark blue-black nighttime sky.
- Procedurally generated stars.
- Animated star twinkling.
- Dawn and dusk color transitions.
- Red-orange sunset coloration.
- Solar disc and atmospheric glow.
- Lunar atmospheric glow.
- Gradual star visibility transitions.
- Independent sky, sun and moon rendering groups.

### Rendering Architecture

- Babylon.js ShaderMaterial handles atmospheric rendering.
- Sky shader receives celestial directions and simulation time.
- Celestial simulation remains the authoritative source of time.
- Sky rendering follows the active camera.
- Celestial lighting remains independent of camera perspective.
- Poincaré projection remains responsible for world geometry.
- The atmospheric sky is a conventional rendering layer and is not transformed through hyperbolic geometry.

### Scene Integration

- CelestialSystem imported by create-scene.js.
- Existing directional sunlight reused.
- Existing hemispheric ambient light reused.
- Moonlight managed by CelestialSystem.
- Celestial instance exposed through scene.metadata.celestial.
- Simulation updated through scene.onBeforeRenderObservable.
- Existing world simulation and rendering loops preserved.

### Configuration

- Day length: 900 seconds.
- Celestial orbit distance: 45,000 units.
- Sky radius: 90,000 units.
- Sun diameter: 1,800 units.
- Moon diameter: 1,300 units.

### Immediate Validation Priorities

1. Verify Babylon.js shader compilation.
2. Verify atmospheric rendering on supported GPUs.
3. Verify sky positioning in flight perspective.
4. Verify sky positioning in third-person perspective.
5. Check rendering order and depth handling.
6. Verify sunrise and sunset coloration.
7. Verify nighttime darkness and star visibility.
8. Verify sun and moon visibility.
9. Check terrain lighting against atmospheric brightness.
10. Check compatibility with GeometryShaderManager.
11. Check cloud rendering against the atmospheric background.
12. Verify that camera switching does not interrupt the celestial simulation.

### Future Atmospheric Improvements

- Physically based atmospheric scattering.
- More realistic solar color based on atmospheric path length.
- Dynamic cloud shadows.
- Volumetric cloud lighting.
- Lunar phases.
- Improved star distribution and constellations.
- Atmospheric haze near the horizon.

### Architectural Requirements

- Preserve existing world and environmental systems.
- Maintain one persistent world across camera modes.
- Keep simulation independent of rendering quality.
- Keep celestial lighting independent of camera perspective.
- Never allow navigation to reach the Poincaré boundary.
- Validate deployed changes before marking proposed features as verified.

### Deployment Status

The atmospheric renderer and scene integration are proposed changes.

Do not consider the new atmospheric rendering milestone
verified until it has been deployed and tested in the actual
Babylon.js application.

Collaboration and Code Delivery Workflow

Future development sessions must follow this workflow:

Inspect the latest publicly available codebase and read the entire current PROJECT-STATE.md.
Inspect existing implementations and dependencies before proposing modifications.
Provide complete replacement files for existing modules that require changes.
Provide complete source files for newly introduced modules.
Include all necessary imports and integration instructions.
Provide PROJECT-STATE.md updates as appendable Markdown.
Clearly distinguish proposed code, delivered code, deployed code and independently verified code.
Do not assume direct repository write access is available. The intended workflow is to deliver complete code additions and replacements directly in the conversation for manual integration.

## Atmospheric Simulation — Milestone 3:
## Regional Weather, Air Pressure and Wind

Implementation prepared: 2026-11-05

### New Module

src/atmosphere/air-pressure-field.js

### Atmospheric Pressure

- Introduced a spatially varying atmospheric pressure field.
- Multiple overlapping pressure waves create large-scale variation.
- Pressure evolves continuously with simulation time.
- Horizontal pressure gradients determine wind direction.
- Prevailing atmospheric circulation provides background wind.
- Wind velocity is constrained to configurable maximum strength.

### Regional Weather Classification

Three atmospheric regimes:

1. Stable
2. Convective
3. Storm-conducive

Classification considers:
- Local evaporation potential.
- Atmospheric pressure.
- An approximate atmospheric instability indicator.

Weather classifications are cached by spatial region.

### Cloud Species Selection

Stable regions:
- Predominantly cumulus.
- Occasional towering cumulus.
- No cumulonimbus.

Convective regions:
- Cumulus.
- Towering cumulus.
- No cumulonimbus.

Storm-conducive regions:
- Cumulus.
- Towering cumulus.
- Rare cumulonimbus.

Similar cloud families are more likely to occur
within the same regional weather system.

### Wind Simulation

- Clouds sample the atmospheric wind field.
- Cloud velocities gradually approach local wind velocity.
- Small-scale atmospheric turbulence is introduced.
- Abrupt changes in wind direction are avoided.
- Clouds retain individual movement velocities.

### Dynamic Cloud Rendering

- Cloud geometry is generated relative to local origins.
- Geometry is cached after generation.
- Current cloud positions are applied during rendering.
- Cloud instance transformations are refreshed periodically.
- Cloud geometry is not regenerated during ordinary movement.

### Performance Considerations

- Pressure field uses inexpensive analytical functions.
- Pressure gradients use four spatial evaluations.
- No full fluid simulation grid introduced.
- No external atmospheric simulation dependencies.
- Weather classifications are cached by region.
- Cloud geometry generation remains independent
  of the wind update frequency.

### Outstanding Atmospheric Work

- Dynamic transitions between weather regimes.
- Atmospheric fronts and storm movement.
- Multiple simultaneous precipitation emitters.
- Cloud shadow projection.
- Volumetric cloud lighting.
- Atmospheric vertical wind profiles.
- Orographic lifting over mountainous terrain.
- Improved pressure and humidity diagnostics.

### Validation Requirements

1. Confirm cumulonimbus formations are uncommon.
2. Confirm cumulonimbus only appear in storm-conducive regions.
3. Confirm neighboring clouds exhibit regional similarity.
4. Confirm pressure changes smoothly across space.
5. Confirm wind direction follows pressure gradients.
6. Confirm clouds move visibly but gently.
7. Confirm rendered cloud positions follow simulation positions.
8. Confirm rainfall still functions.
9. Confirm cloud rendering works in both camera perspectives.
10. Measure CPU and GPU performance.

### Deployment Status

Source changes prepared for manual integration.

Deployment and runtime verification pending.

Terrain Worker

Identified defect:

Duplicate terrainColor() declarations in src/world/terrain-mesh-worker.js.
The second declaration overrides the complete implementation.
Its commented-out color assignments leave color undefined.
This causes terrain generation exceptions.

Proposed correction:

Remove the defective duplicate implementation.
Preserve the complete geological terrain-color implementation.
Improve worker initialization diagnostics.
Add worker initialization timeout.
Handle worker runtime and message deserialization failures.
Preserve outstanding valid tasks during worker failure.
Improve synchronous fallback queue handling.
Celestial Rendering

Primary module:

src/environment/celestial-system.js

Identified issues:

Sky dome combines infinite-distance rendering with manual camera positioning.
Sky radius approaches the camera far clipping distance.
Sun and moon are independently positioned camera-relative meshes.
Celestial meshes render independently of normal scene depth occlusion.

Proposed corrections:

Use conventional camera-centered sky positioning.
Constrain effective sky radius to the camera far plane.
Render solar and lunar discs through the atmospheric shader.
Remove independent celestial meshes.
Preserve directional sunlight and moonlight simulation.
Preserve dawn, dusk, nighttime and star transitions.
Preserve atmospheric glow.

Bug Fix — Celestial System Initialization

Implementation prepared: 2026-11-05

Identified Defect

Module: src/environment/celestial-system.js

Application initialization failed with:

TypeError: can't access property "setVector3", this.skyMaterial is undefined

Root cause:

The CelestialSystem constructor invoked update(0) before creating the atmospheric sky.
skyMaterial was initialized exclusively inside createSky().
The constructor did not invoke createSky().
Consequently, the initial update attempted to access an undefined shader material.
Proposed Corrections
Initialize the atmospheric sky through createSky() before the initial simulation update.
Preserve initialization of the authoritative solar and lunar direction vectors.
Retain shader-based rendering of the sun and moon.
Avoid redundant independent celestial meshes.
Make disposal safe when rendering resources have not been fully initialized.
Preserve existing directional sunlight and hemispheric ambient illumination.
Preserve camera-relative sky positioning.
Preserve the existing day/night cycle and atmospheric transitions.
Initialization Sequence
Initialize celestial simulation state.
Initialize directional lighting.
Initialize the atmospheric sky mesh and shader material.
Initialize atmospheric shader uniforms.
Perform the initial celestial update.
Begin normal scene-driven simulation updates.
Validation Requirements
Confirm application initialization completes.
Confirm atmospheric shader compilation.
Confirm no undefined sky-material errors.
Confirm sun and moon visibility.
Confirm camera-relative sky positioning.
Test flight perspective.
Test third-person perspective.
Verify sunrise and sunset transitions.
Verify nighttime star visibility.
Verify scene disposal without errors.
Confirm compatibility with GeometryShaderManager.
Confirm terrain and cloud rendering remain unaffected.
Deployment Status

Source correction proposed for manual integration.

Not yet deployed or independently runtime verified.

Do not mark this correction as verified until tested in the running Babylon.js application.
Environmental Generation — Soil Distribution Map

Implementation prepared: 2026-11-05

Objective

Introduce geographically coherent soil distribution using the existing topographic heightmap as a shared soil-distribution resource.

Texture Architecture

Existing resource:

assets/0_somnium_map_topographic.png

The same texture supplies:

Terrain elevation.
Broad soil distribution.

Pixel brightness determines soil classification through configurable normalized brightness ranges.

Initial Soil Classification
Brightness	Soil
0.00–0.18	Clay / wet sediment
0.18–0.38	Desert sand
0.38–0.58	Fertile soil / sandy loam
0.58–0.76	Loam
0.76–0.90	Organic soil
0.90–1.00	Scree / exposed geological material

Desert sand receives a distinctive golden-yellow surface coloration.

These thresholds are initial tuning parameters and require visual validation against the actual topographic texture.

Modified Modules
src/environment/heightmap-terrain.js
Introduce normalized map-brightness sampling.
Preserve existing bilinear coordinate interpolation.
src/geology/geological-field.js
Use topographic brightness for regional soil classification.
Preserve local geological and moisture variation.
Preserve soil horizons and material properties.
Expose map brightness in geological diagnostics.
src/world/terrain-mesh-worker.js
Apply matching soil classification to terrain vertex colors.
Preserve bedrock blending and surface variation.
Remove the defective duplicate terrainColor implementation.

Hydrological infiltration.
Soil water retention.
Drainage.
Soil fertility.
Ecological suitability.
Future biome classification.

The desert distribution thresholds must be visually tuned against the actual topographic map before the milestone is considered complete.

Objective

Introduce multiple independently named Somnium worlds with persistent storage, independent procedural seeds and serialized ecological populations.

The architecture is intended to support hybrid procedural/manual world creation.

Architectural Decisions
Multiple named worlds are supported from the beginning.

World data is schema-versioned.

Current Scope Limitations

## Architectural Editor Development

### Architectural principles

- Building placement is independent of terrain elevation.
- Horizontal positioning, vertical elevation, and rotation
  are independently controllable.
- Terrain is a spatial reference, not a placement constraint.
- Buildings may be underground, underwater, floating,
  or positioned above terrain.
- Building geometry remains independent of editor UI.
- Reference images are independent assets, not geometry.
- Architectural components should remain modular and reusable.
- Character placement and editing are future milestones.

### Coordinate systems

- Authoritative world coordinates are distinct from
  Babylon rendering coordinates.
- SpatialTransform is responsible for conversions.
- Rendering systems must not assume world coordinates
  are directly interchangeable with render coordinates.
- Nonlinear projections require appropriate local
  geometric approximations.

### Rendering

- Prefer thin-instance rendering for repeated geometry.
- Reuse geometry prototypes and materials.
- Avoid unnecessary mesh creation.
- Keep rendering independent of architectural data.
- Consider projection-aware geometry for large structures.

### Editor architecture

- EditorCamera manages architectural navigation.
- BuildingPlacement manages placement transforms.
- FloorPlanReference manages imported reference imagery.
- BuildingEditor coordinates interaction and presentation.
- BuildingSystem remains responsible for building data
  and procedural geometry generation.

### Game modes

- Flight, third-person, and architectural editing are
  independent application modes.
- Camera state must be preserved when switching modes.
- Editor controls must not interfere with gameplay controls.

### Source-code collection convention

Whenever requesting source code from the developer,
provide a copyable Bash command that:

1. Lists every requested filename explicitly.
2. Reads each complete file.
3. Adds clear filename delimiters.
4. Sends the combined output to wl-copy.
5. Requires no manual filename-list assembly.

Example:

    printf '%s\n' \
      src/example/one.js \
      src/example/two.js \
      | while IFS= read -r f; do
          [[ -f "$f" ]] && {
            printf '\n\n===== %s =====\n' "$f"
            cat "$f"
          }
        done | wl-copy

Code Delivery and Collaboration — Updated Requirements
Complete File Delivery

When modifying an existing source file, provide the complete replacement file rather than isolated snippets or patches.

This applies even when the modification is very small.

Maximum Code Block Size

No individual code block may exceed 200 lines.

When a complete file exceeds 200 lines, divide it into consecutively numbered sections.

Each section must:

Identify the destination filename.
Identify its position within the complete file.
Preserve the original line order.
Be directly concatenable with the preceding and following sections.

The sections together must constitute the complete replacement file.

Large Codebase Analysis

Do not request or exchange the entire codebase unnecessarily.

Instead:

Inspect the published source and file tree.
Identify the modules directly involved in the current task.
Trace their dependencies and integration points.
Request additional source only when a specific uncertainty remains.
Prefer targeted investigation over repeated full-codebase transfers.
Project Status Updates

When substantial implementation work is delivered, provide an appendable update for PROJECT-STATE.txt.

Every update must distinguish:

Proposed implementation.
Code delivered.
Code manually integrated.
Code deployed.
Runtime verified functionality.

Never describe untested functionality as verified.

Integration and Verification

Include explicit integration instructions and practical verification steps.

For rendering changes, verify actual visible output rather than relying exclusively on successful generation messages or instance counts.

Architectural Rendering Improvements

Identified duplicate spatial projection in the architectural rendering pipeline.

Previous behavior:

BuildingRenderer transformed component positions and dimensions.
ThinInstanceRenderer passed the resulting matrices to GeometryRenderer.
GeometryRenderer performed another spatial projection.

Corrected behavior:

BuildingGenerator produces authoritative architectural geometry.
BuildingRenderer constructs matrices in authoritative world coordinates.
ThinInstanceRenderer delegates spatial projection.
GeometryRenderer performs the projection once.

Existing thin-instance rendering and architectural material buckets are preserved.

Existing BuildingModel serialization and BuildingSystem world capture/restoration remain unchanged.

Architectural components continue to be stored independently of the original floor-plan image.

Building Placement Lifecycle

Introduced two building states:

Provisional
Committed

Provisional buildings:

Are visible in the environment.
Can be repositioned and rotated.
Can have their architectural dimensions adjusted.
Receive temporary visual highlighting.
Are excluded from persistent world capture.

Committed buildings:

Remain visible in the environment.
Lose their temporary highlighting.
Are included in world persistence.
Remain available when another building is constructed.

After placement, the editor releases the current building selection and prepares for another floor-plan import.

Aspect Ratio Correction

The floor-plan editor now supports maintaining the source image's aspect ratio.

When enabled:

Width remains the primary dimension.
Depth is calculated from the original image dimensions.
The displayed reference image and generated architectural geometry receive identical dimensions.

Manual depth adjustment disables automatic aspect-ratio locking.

Architectural Wall Dimensions

Wall height:

Default: 4 units.
Minimum: 2.4 units.
Maximum: 1,000 units.

Wall thickness:

Default: 0.35 units.
Minimum: 0.1 units.
Maximum: 1,000 units.

Wall height and thickness are independently configurable.

Persistence Changes

BuildingSystem.captureWorld() excludes provisional buildings.

Committed buildings continue using the existing BuildingModel serialization interface.

No separate preview entities are introduced into persistent world data.

Architectural Preservation
Existing building generation retained.
```

```
SOMNIUM
Software Architecture & Development Style Guide

Version: 1.0
Status: Foundational / Progressive Adoption
Last updated: November 5, 2026

1. Foundational Philosophy

Somnium is intended to be a continuously extensible, technology-agnostic, high-performance interactive world system.

Its architecture should prioritize:

Abstraction.
Modularity.
Extensibility.
Separation of concerns.
Reusability.
Performance.
Robustness.
Persistence and reproducibility.
Interoperability.
Maintainability.

These principles should guide every new subsystem and every substantial modification to an existing subsystem.

1.1 Technology independence

Application architecture should describe what systems do, not how a particular technology accomplishes those tasks.

Examples:

Rendering systems should not determine authoritative world state.
Physics implementations should not own application-level entity definitions.
Persistence systems should not require knowledge of rendering objects.
User interfaces should communicate through stable application interfaces.
Procedural generation should not depend on a particular rendering engine.
Simulation should not depend on a particular visualization technique.

Technology-specific implementations should be replaceable without requiring widespread changes to unrelated systems.

1.2 Abstraction without unnecessary complexity

Always seek the highest useful level of abstraction.

However, abstraction should produce a concrete architectural benefit.

Do not introduce abstractions merely to increase the number of interfaces, classes, or files.

An abstraction is valuable when it:

Reduces coupling.
Enables interchangeable implementations.
Establishes reusable behavior.
Simplifies testing.
Protects authoritative data.
Enables optimization.
Makes future capabilities easier to introduce.

Prefer simple, composable abstractions over elaborate inheritance hierarchies.

1.3 Progressive architectural improvement

The codebase will evolve incrementally.

Existing implementations need not immediately conform to every principle in this document.

New code should follow the guide wherever practical. Existing code should be progressively refactored when it is modified or when architectural deficiencies become significant.

Avoid large-scale rewrites without a demonstrated benefit.

2. Architectural Organization
2.1 Separation of responsibilities

Organize the application into distinct conceptual layers.

Domain layer

Contains authoritative application concepts and their rules.

Examples:

Buildings.
Rooms.
Characters.
Terrain.
Vegetation.
World entities.
Materials.
Spatial relationships.

Domain objects should remain independent of external libraries.

Application layer

Coordinates domain operations and application workflows.

Examples:

Building creation.
World loading.
Entity management.
Procedural generation.
Scene initialization.
User commands.
Simulation orchestration.
Infrastructure layer

Provides implementation-specific services.

Examples:

Rendering engines.
Database adapters.
Browser storage.
Asset loading.
Network communication.
Physics engines.
File access.
Presentation layer

Handles user interaction and visualization.

Examples:

Editors.
Menus.
HUDs.
View controls.
Selection interfaces.
Debugging tools.

Dependencies should generally point inward, toward stable concepts, rather than outward toward specific implementations.

2.2 Dependency inversion

Higher-level systems should depend on capabilities rather than concrete implementations wherever useful.

For example:

A building system should request architectural rendering through a rendering interface.

It should not need to know whether the implementation uses:

Thin instances.
Ordinary meshes.
GPU instancing.
Generated geometry.
A future alternative rendering engine.

The same principle applies to persistence, physics, navigation, and asset management.

2.3 Explicit ownership

Every resource and responsibility should have an identifiable owner.

Establish clear ownership for:

Entity lifecycle.
Geometry generation.
Rendering resources.
Materials.
Input subscriptions.
Event listeners.
Persistent records.
Temporary editor objects.
Background tasks.

Avoid multiple systems independently managing the same resource.

Resource creation and disposal should follow symmetrical lifecycle rules.

3. Data Architecture
3.1 Authoritative data versus derived data

Maintain a strict distinction between authoritative data and derived representations.

Authoritative data describes what exists.

Derived data describes how that information is represented or processed.

For example:

Authoritative data	Derived representation
Building definition	Babylon.js meshes
Room boundaries	Rendered walls
Character state	Animated skeleton
Terrain definition	Terrain chunk geometry
World entity records	Thin-instance buffers
Material definition	GPU material resources
Navigation graph	Pathfinding acceleration structures

Derived representations must be reproducible from authoritative information wherever practical.

Never make an ephemeral rendering resource the sole source of truth for important application state.

3.2 Serializable domain models

Persistent domain objects should use plain, versioned data structures.

Prefer:

Strings.
Numbers.
Booleans.
Arrays.
Plain objects.
Explicit identifiers.
Well-defined optional properties.

Avoid serializing:

Engine objects.
Functions.
Circular references.
DOM elements.
GPU resources.
Runtime caches.
Temporary interaction state.

Every persistent model should have an explicit serialization and restoration strategy.

3.3 Stable identity

Every persistent entity should have a stable identifier.

Identifiers should survive:

Saving.
Loading.
Duplication, with new identity.
World transitions.
Application restarts.

Do not rely on array indices, object references, mesh names, or rendering instance indices as persistent identity.

3.4 Versioning and migration

Persistent data formats should be explicitly versioned.

Changes to data models should provide a migration strategy.

Prefer backward-compatible additions where practical.

A migration should be:

Deterministic.
Explicit.
Testable.
Idempotent where possible.

Never silently reinterpret existing persistent data.

3.5 Data integrity

Validate information at system boundaries.

Important validation targets include:

Numeric ranges.
Spatial dimensions.
Entity references.
Identifier uniqueness.
Required fields.
Enumerated values.
Referential integrity.
Version compatibility.

Invalid data should produce actionable errors rather than silently propagating through the application.

4. Spatial Architecture

Spatial representation is a foundational concern throughout Somnium.

4.1 Coordinate-system independence

World coordinates must be distinguished from rendering coordinates.

The authoritative world representation should remain independent of a particular projection, camera, or renderer.

Somnium's SpatialTransform architecture is an important example of this principle.

Spatial transformations should be centralized rather than independently implemented by individual systems.

4.2 Transformation responsibilities

Maintain explicit distinctions between:

Local coordinates.
Parent-relative coordinates.
World coordinates.
Rendering coordinates.
Camera-relative coordinates.
Screen coordinates.

Transformations between these spaces should be explicit and consistently named.

Avoid implicit coordinate conversions.

4.3 Projection independence

Systems should not assume that the world is necessarily Euclidean.

Support the possibility of:

Euclidean geometry.
Hyperbolic geometry.
Alternative projections.
Local coordinate frames.
Future nonstandard spatial representations.

Rendering-specific approximations must not silently become authoritative physical or architectural rules.

4.4 Spatial precision

Establish consistent numerical precision requirements.

Consider:

Floating-point accumulation.
Large coordinate magnitudes.
Transformation stability.
Geometric tolerances.
Projection distortion.
Collision tolerances.

Use shared precision and tolerance policies rather than arbitrary constants scattered throughout the codebase.

5. Rendering Architecture
5.1 Rendering is a replaceable subsystem

Rendering should consume application data and produce visual representations.

It should not own authoritative domain state.

The application should remain conceptually functional even if its current rendering implementation is replaced.

5.2 Geometry reuse

Prefer reusable geometry and shared resources.

Avoid unnecessary duplication of:

Mesh prototypes.
Materials.
Textures.
Shader programs.
Geometry buffers.
Vertex data.

Rendering systems should favor batching and instancing when appropriate.

5.3 Instancing and thin instances

Somnium should prioritize efficient rendering of repeated geometry.

Thin instances are an important current optimization.

However, rendering architecture should support multiple strategies, including:

Thin instances.
Hardware instancing.
Shared meshes.
Geometry merging.
Procedural geometry.
Level-of-detail representations.
GPU-driven rendering.

Choose strategies based on actual workload characteristics.

Do not force unique or highly dynamic objects into an instancing architecture when doing so would create unnecessary complexity.

5.4 Incremental updates

Avoid rebuilding unchanged resources.

Prefer:

Dirty-state tracking.
Incremental buffer updates.
Cached geometry.
Shared prototypes.
Deferred regeneration.
Change notifications.
Explicit invalidation.

An edit to one building should not unnecessarily regenerate every building in the world.

5.5 Rendering and simulation independence

Rendering frequency should not determine simulation correctness.

Simulation state should remain authoritative independently of frame rate.

Rendering may interpolate or approximate simulation state without modifying it.

6. Performance Engineering

Performance is an architectural requirement, not a final optimization pass.

6.1 Optimize at the correct level

Prioritize improvements in this order:

Eliminate unnecessary work.
Reduce duplicated data.
Reduce expensive allocations.
Reduce unnecessary synchronization.
Improve algorithmic complexity.
Improve batching and resource reuse.
Optimize low-level operations.

Avoid premature micro-optimization when a larger architectural improvement is available.

6.2 Resource lifecycle

Resources should have explicit creation, reuse, invalidation, and disposal policies.

Avoid:

Repeated object allocation in hot loops.
Unbounded caches.
Orphaned event listeners.
Unnecessary GPU uploads.
Repeated geometry generation.
Uncontrolled asynchronous operations.
6.3 Scalability

New systems should consider their behavior at substantially larger scales than initial testing requires.

Consider:

Thousands of entities.
Large terrain regions.
Many buildings.
Complex interiors.
Numerous characters.
Large numbers of repeated assets.

Use spatial partitioning, chunking, streaming, culling, and level-of-detail systems where appropriate.

6.4 Measure before optimizing

Performance changes should be supported by measurements.

Track relevant metrics such as:

Frame time.
CPU utilization.
GPU utilization.
Draw calls.
Instance counts.
Memory consumption.
Geometry regeneration time.
Loading time.
Serialization time.

Do not assume an optimization is successful merely because the implementation appears more sophisticated.

7. System Design and Extensibility
7.1 Modular systems

Each subsystem should have a clearly defined responsibility and a relatively small public interface.

Examples:

BuildingSystem.
TerrainSystem.
CharacterSystem.
VegetationSystem.
PhysicsSystem.
NavigationSystem.
PersistenceSystem.

Systems should communicate through explicit interfaces and events rather than reaching into one another's internal implementation details.

7.2 Composition over inheritance

Prefer composition when assembling complex entities and behaviors.

For example, a character may consist of:

Identity.
Transform.
Appearance.
Animation.
Physics.
Navigation.
Interaction capabilities.

These capabilities should be independently extensible.

Avoid deep inheritance trees that make unrelated functionality difficult to modify.

7.3 Capability-based architecture

Where appropriate, describe entities in terms of the capabilities they provide.

For example, a door might provide:

An architectural opening.
An interaction target.
An obstruction.
A navigation connection.
An animation controller.

Other systems should consume the relevant capabilities without depending on the entire door implementation.

7.4 Event-driven communication

Use events for meaningful changes in system state.

Examples:

EntityCreated.
EntityRemoved.
BuildingModified.
RoomConnected.
WorldLoaded.
WorldSaved.
TransformChanged.
AssetLoaded.

Events should have documented payloads and predictable delivery semantics.

Avoid excessive event indirection when a direct function call is simpler and clearer.

7.5 Avoid hidden dependencies

Systems should not rely on undocumented global variables, implicit initialization order, or unrelated modules modifying shared state.

Dependencies should be explicit and injectable where practical.

8. Procedural Generation

Procedural generation should be independent of the rendering process.

8.1 Determinism

Where practical, generation should be reproducible from:

Input parameters.
Random seeds.
Versioned algorithms.
Relevant world state.

The same inputs should produce equivalent results.

8.2 Generation pipeline

Prefer a staged pipeline:

Input interpretation.
Domain-level generation.
Validation.
Structural representation.
Optimization.
Rendering conversion.
Runtime integration.

This makes generators easier to test and reuse.

8.3 Non-destructive generation

Generated content should remain identifiable as generated content.

Where practical, preserve the distinction between:

Generated structures.
User modifications.
Manually placed entities.
Derived rendering resources.

Do not overwrite user-authored information during regeneration without an explicit policy.

9. User Interfaces and Interaction
9.1 Presentation independence

User interfaces should interact with application systems through stable commands and interfaces.

The domain model should not depend on DOM elements or UI-specific state.

9.2 Input abstraction

Pointer, keyboard, touch, and future controller input should ultimately feed a common interaction architecture.

Avoid embedding application behavior directly into individual event handlers.

9.3 Interaction feedback

Every meaningful operation should provide appropriate feedback.

Examples:

Successful placement.
Invalid placement.
Missing selection.
Failed picking.
Saving.
Loading.
Resource unavailability.

Never silently discard a user operation without a meaningful explanation.

9.4 Editing workflows

Editors should distinguish:

Selection.
Preview.
Modification.
Validation.
Commitment.
Cancellation.

Temporary preview objects should not become authoritative domain entities until the user commits an operation.

9.5 Undo and redo

Substantial editing systems should progressively adopt command-based operations.

An operation should ideally support:

Execution.
Validation.
Reversal.
Reapplication.
Serialization where appropriate.

This will be particularly valuable for architectural editing.

10. Error Handling and Robustness
10.1 Fail explicitly

Errors should be observable, contextual, and actionable.

Avoid empty catch blocks and silent failure.

Errors should identify:

The operation.
The affected entity.
The relevant subsystem.
The underlying cause.
10.2 Defensive boundaries

Validate external inputs and persistent data.

Do not unnecessarily duplicate validation throughout trusted internal operations.

Establish clear trust boundaries.

10.3 Graceful degradation

Optional capabilities should not cause unrelated application systems to fail.

Examples:

Missing decorative assets should not prevent world loading.
Rendering failures should not destroy persistent data.
Optional editor tools should not prevent gameplay.
An unavailable external service should not corrupt local state.
10.4 Lifecycle safety

Asynchronous operations should account for disposal, cancellation, and changing application state.

Avoid applying results to objects that have already been destroyed or replaced.

11. Coding Conventions
11.1 General conventions
Use modern JavaScript modules.
Prefer const over let where reassignment is unnecessary.
Avoid var.
Use descriptive identifiers.
Prefer explicit object structures over positional arguments when parameters become numerous.
Use small, focused functions.
Keep side effects identifiable.
Avoid unnecessary global state.
Avoid magic numbers.
Centralize shared configuration.
Document non-obvious architectural decisions.
11.2 Naming

Use consistent terminology throughout the codebase.

Prefer:

createBuilding()
serializeBuilding()
restoreWorld()
generateGeometry()
updateInstances()

Avoid multiple names for the same conceptual operation.

Class names should describe responsibilities.

Functions should generally describe actions.

Boolean variables should read naturally as conditions.

11.3 Constants and configuration

Avoid scattering implementation-specific values throughout source files.

Centralize values such as:

Default dimensions.
Spatial tolerances.
Rendering limits.
Material definitions.
Performance thresholds.
Feature flags.

Distinguish configuration from runtime state.

11.4 Comments and documentation

Comments should explain reasoning, constraints, and non-obvious behavior.

Do not use comments merely to restate the code.

Document public interfaces, important invariants, and lifecycle requirements.

Architectural decisions with long-term consequences should be recorded.

12. Testing and Verification
12.1 Layered testing

Where practical, provide:

Unit tests.
Integration tests.
Serialization tests.
Geometry validation tests.
Rendering tests.
Performance tests.
End-to-end interaction tests.
12.2 Independent verification

A successful function call does not prove that an entire feature works.

For example, successful building creation does not prove:

Geometry is visible.
Thin instances are correctly transformed.
Persistence works.
Openings are correctly generated.
The building survives world reload.

Test complete workflows.

12.3 Regression protection

Every substantial change should consider its effects on existing systems.

Particular attention should be paid to:

World streaming.
Persistence.
Terrain generation.
Spatial transformations.
Rendering.
Existing UI.
Scene lifecycle.
12.4 Verification honesty

Clearly distinguish:

Designed.
Implemented.
Integrated.
Tested.
Runtime verified.
Deployed.

Never claim functionality is verified merely because source code has been written.

13. Security and Data Ownership
Treat externally supplied data as untrusted.
Validate imported world records.
Avoid unsafe dynamic code execution.
Minimize unnecessary permissions.
Keep secrets out of source code and persistent world records.
Avoid exposing internal implementation state through public interfaces.
Establish clear ownership of user-generated content.
Preserve data integrity during interrupted operations.
14. Project Documentation

Documentation should evolve alongside the codebase.

Maintain:

PROJECT-STATE.md

The authoritative record of:

Existing architecture.
Implemented systems.
Current development status.
Known limitations.
Pending work.
Integration requirements.
Verification status.
STYLE-GUIDE.md

The architectural and coding standards defined in this document.

Architecture decision records

For consequential decisions, record:

Context.
Problem.
Considered alternatives.
Decision.
Consequences.
Future reconsideration criteria.
Module documentation

Important systems should document:

Purpose.
Public interfaces.
Dependencies.
Data ownership.
Lifecycle.
Known limitations.

Documentation should describe the actual codebase, not an aspirational implementation.

15. Progressive Refactoring Strategy

The style guide should be adopted incrementally.

Prioritize improvements according to their architectural impact.

Priority 1 — Architectural boundaries

Establish clear separation between:

Domain data.
Application logic.
Rendering.
Persistence.
Presentation.
Priority 2 — Data integrity

Standardize:

Persistent models.
Identifiers.
Serialization.
Validation.
Versioning.
Priority 3 — Spatial consistency

Standardize:

Coordinate systems.
Transform interfaces.
Projection boundaries.
Spatial tolerances.
Priority 4 — Resource management

Improve:

Rendering resource ownership.
Instancing.
Incremental updates.
Caching.
Disposal.
Priority 5 — Inter-system communication

Introduce:

Explicit interfaces.
Consistent lifecycle management.
Shared event conventions.
Capability-based composition.
Priority 6 — Optimization and scalability

Measure and progressively improve:

CPU performance.
GPU performance.
Memory usage.
Streaming.
Geometry generation.
Persistence performance.
Priority 7 — Comprehensive testing

Build automated verification around increasingly complex system interactions.

16. Architectural Decision Principles

When making significant implementation decisions, evaluate them against the following questions:

Does this preserve separation of concerns?
Does it reduce unnecessary coupling?
Can the implementation be replaced independently?
Is authoritative data protected?
Can the system be reused elsewhere?
Does it scale beyond its initial use case?
Does it support efficient execution?
Is its lifecycle explicit?
Can it be independently tested?
Does it preserve existing functionality?
Is the abstraction justified by a concrete benefit?
Does it make future development easier rather than merely moving complexity elsewhere?

If a decision violates an existing principle, document the reason.

Practical constraints may justify exceptions, but exceptions should be deliberate rather than accidental.

17. Long-Term Architectural Vision

Somnium should progressively develop toward a collection of independent, composable systems operating on shared authoritative world data.

The same architectural foundations should support:

Procedural world generation.
Terrain and environmental simulation.
Vegetation and ecological systems.
Architectural construction.
Interior environments.
Furniture and object libraries.
Character creation.
Character behavior and navigation.
Physics and interactions.
Lighting and atmosphere.
Persistent simulation.
Multiplayer synchronization.
Alternative rendering implementations.
Alternative spatial representations.

These capabilities should emerge from shared architectural foundations rather than isolated, incompatible implementations.

The long-term goal is not to create the most elaborate framework possible.

It is to create the simplest coherent architecture capable of supporting increasingly sophisticated functionality.

18. Final Guiding Principle

Build systems around enduring concepts, not temporary implementations.

Prefer:

Data over incidental representation.
Composition over rigid inheritance.
Interfaces over concrete dependencies.
Reusable capabilities over duplicated functionality.
Deterministic processes over hidden state.
Explicit ownership over implicit coordination.
Incremental updates over unnecessary reconstruction.
Measured optimization over speculation.
Reversible decisions over unnecessary architectural commitments.

Every new feature should strengthen the existing architecture rather than create another isolated technical ecosystem.

Every substantial modification should leave the codebase at least as coherent, understandable, and extensible as it was before.

Somnium should grow in capability without growing proportionally in architectural complexity.
```

src/atmosphere/cloud-system.js
_______
```

import { Cloud } from "./cloud.js";

import { CloudSpecies } from "./cloud-species.js";

import { CloudGenerator } from "./cloud-generator.js";

import { AirPressureField } from "./air-pressure-field.js";

import { CloudRenderer } from "../rendering/cloud-renderer.js";


class CloudSystem {
    constructor(
        scene,
        terrain,
        waterSystem,
        hydrology,
        rainSystem
    ) {
        this.scene = scene;

        this.terrain = terrain;
        this.waterSystem = waterSystem;
        this.hydrology = hydrology;
        this.rainSystem = rainSystem;

        this.clouds = [];

        this.renderer = new CloudRenderer(scene);

        this.maxClouds = 100;

        this.rainingCloud = null;

        // Atmospheric circulation.
        this.pressureField = new AirPressureField();

        this.elapsed = 0;

        // Regional weather classification.
        this.weatherCellSize = 100000;

        this.weatherCells = new Map();

        // Rendering update frequency.
        this.renderAccumulator = 0;
        this.renderInterval = 0.4;

        this.renderDirty = false;

        this.disposed = false;
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // REGIONAL WEATHER
    // ------------------------------------------

    getWeatherCellKey(x, z) {
        const cx = Math.floor(
            x / this.weatherCellSize
        );

        const cz = Math.floor(
            z / this.weatherCellSize
        );

        return `${cx},${cz}`;
    }

    getWeatherRegime(position) {
        const key = this.getWeatherCellKey(
            position.x,
            position.z
        );

        if (this.weatherCells.has(key)) {
            return this.weatherCells.get(key);
        }

        const centerX =
            (Math.floor(
                position.x / this.weatherCellSize
            ) + 0.5) * this.weatherCellSize;

        const centerZ =
            (Math.floor(
                position.z / this.weatherCellSize
            ) + 0.5) * this.weatherCellSize;

        const center = new BABYLON.Vector3(
            centerX,
            position.y,
            centerZ
        );

        const atmospheric =
            this.pressureField.sample(center);

        const moisture = BABYLON.Scalar.Clamp(
            this.waterSystem.getEvaporationPotential(
                center
            ),
            0,
            1
        );

        // Lower pressure favors rising air.
        // Moisture is required for deep convection.
        const instability = BABYLON.Scalar.Clamp(
            0.5 - atmospheric.pressure,
            0,
            1
        );

        let regime = "stable";

        if (
            moisture >= 0.58 &&
            atmospheric.pressure <= -0.12 &&
            instability >= 0.62
        ) {
            regime = "storm";
        } else if (
            moisture >= 0.36 &&
            atmospheric.pressure <= 0.20
        ) {
            regime = "convective";
        }

        this.weatherCells.set(key, regime);

        return regime;
    }

    selectSpecies(regime, random = Math.random) {
        const value = random();

        switch (regime) {
            case "storm":
                if (value < 0.20) {
                    return "cumulus";
                }

                if (value < 0.80) {
                    return "towering";
                }

                return "cumulonimbus";

            case "convective":
                return value < 0.45
                    ? "cumulus"
                    : "towering";

            default:
                return value < 0.85
                    ? "cumulus"
                    : "towering";
        }
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // CLOUD GENERATION
    // ------------------------------------------

    generate(count = 35) {
        if (this.disposed) return;

        this.clouds = [];

        this.weatherCells.clear();

        this.renderer.clear();

        const actualCount = Math.min(
            count,
            this.maxClouds
        );

        for (
            let i = 0;
            i < actualCount;
            i++
        ) {
            const terrainPosition =
                this.terrain.sample();

            if (!terrainPosition) continue;

            const weatherRegime =
                this.getWeatherRegime(
                    terrainPosition
                );

            const speciesName =
                this.selectSpecies(weatherRegime);

            const config =
                CloudSpecies[speciesName];

            const scale =
                config.scale *
                (
                    speciesName === "cumulonimbus"
                        ? 0.78 + Math.random() * 0.35
                        : 0.70 + Math.random() * 0.55
                );

            const position =
                terrainPosition.add(
                    new BABYLON.Vector3(
                        0,
                        90 + Math.random() * 65,
                        0
                    )
                );

            const cloud = new Cloud(
                position,
                speciesName,
                scale,
                config,
                weatherRegime
            );

            // Generate the formation relative to
            // its own origin, not world coordinates.
            const generator = new CloudGenerator(
                config
            );

            cloud.geometryMatrices =
                generator.generate(
                    BABYLON.Vector3.Zero(),
                    scale,
                    config.recursionDepth,
                    i + 91827
                );

            // Start with the prevailing wind.
            const atmospheric =
                this.pressureField.sample(position);

            cloud.velocity.copyFrom(
                atmospheric.wind.scale(0.35)
            );

            this.clouds.push(cloud);
        }

        this.updateRendering(true);
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // WIND AND CLOUD MOVEMENT
    // ------------------------------------------

    updateWind(cloud, deltaTime) {
        const atmospheric =
            this.pressureField.sample(
                cloud.position
            );

        const target = atmospheric.wind;

        // Gradually approach the local wind velocity.
        // This avoids abrupt changes between regions.
        const response = 1 - Math.exp(
            -cloud.windResponse * deltaTime
        );

        cloud.velocity.x +=
            (target.x - cloud.velocity.x) *
            response;

        cloud.velocity.z +=
            (target.z - cloud.velocity.z) *
            response;

        // Small, smooth atmospheric turbulence.
        const turbulence =
            cloud.windTurbulence;

        const phase = this.elapsed * 0.12;

        cloud.velocity.x +=
            Math.sin(phase + cloud.position.z * 0.0001) *
            turbulence *
            deltaTime;

        cloud.velocity.z +=
            Math.cos(phase + cloud.position.x * 0.0001) *
            turbulence *
            deltaTime;

        cloud.position.x +=
            cloud.velocity.x * deltaTime * 0.5;

        cloud.position.z +=
            cloud.velocity.z * deltaTime * 0.5;
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // CLOUD SIMULATION
    // ------------------------------------------

    update(deltaYears) {
        if (this.disposed) return;

        const deltaTime = Math.max(
            0,
            Math.min(deltaYears, 0.25)
        );

        this.elapsed += deltaTime;

        this.pressureField.update(deltaTime);

        let activeRainCloud = null;

        for (const cloud of this.clouds) {
            this.updateCloud(cloud, deltaTime);

            if (
                cloud.isRaining &&
                (
                    !activeRainCloud ||
                    cloud.rainIntensity >
                    activeRainCloud.rainIntensity
                )
            ) {
                activeRainCloud = cloud;
            }
        }

        this.rainingCloud = activeRainCloud;

        if (activeRainCloud) {
            const rainPosition =
                activeRainCloud.position.clone();

            rainPosition.y -= 10;

            this.rainSystem.start(
                rainPosition,
                activeRainCloud.rainIntensity
            );
        } else {
            this.rainSystem.stop();
        }

        this.renderAccumulator += deltaTime;

        if (
            this.renderDirty &&
            this.renderAccumulator >=
                this.renderInterval
        ) {
            this.updateRendering();

            this.renderAccumulator = 0;
        }
    }

    updateCloud(cloud, deltaTime) {
        this.updateWind(cloud, deltaTime);

        // Cache environmental sampling.
        cloud.environmentSampleTimer -= deltaTime;

        if (cloud.environmentSampleTimer <= 0) {
            cloud.evaporationPotential =
                BABYLON.Scalar.Clamp(
                    this.waterSystem.getEvaporationPotential(
                        cloud.position
                    ),
                    0,
                    1
                );

            cloud.environmentSampleTimer = 1.0;
        }

        // Moisture accumulation.
        if (!cloud.isRaining) {
            cloud.waterContent +=
                cloud.absorptionRate *
                cloud.evaporationPotential *
                deltaTime;
        }

        cloud.waterContent = Math.min(
            cloud.waterContent,
            cloud.maxWaterCapacity
        );

        // Precipitation threshold.
        if (
            cloud.waterContent >=
            cloud.maxWaterCapacity
        ) {
            this.startRain(cloud);
        }

        // Rainfall duration.
        if (cloud.isRaining) {
            cloud.rainRemaining -= deltaTime;

            if (cloud.rainRemaining <= 0) {
                cloud.isRaining = false;
                cloud.rainIntensity = 0;
                cloud.rainRemaining = 0;
            }
        }

        this.renderDirty = true;
    }

    startRain(cloud) {
        cloud.isRaining = true;

        cloud.rainRemaining =
            cloud.rainDuration;

        cloud.rainIntensity =
            0.5 + cloud.moistureRatio * 1.5;

        const releasedWater =
            cloud.maxWaterCapacity * 0.45;

        cloud.waterContent -= releasedWater;

        const rainfallAmount =
            0.25 * cloud.rainIntensity;

        const rainfallRadius =
            cloud.scale * 4;

        this.hydrology.addRain(
            cloud.position,
            rainfallRadius,
            rainfallAmount
        );
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // RENDERING
    // ------------------------------------------

    updateRendering(force = false) {
        if (
            !force &&
            !this.renderDirty
        ) {
            return;
        }

        this.renderer.clear();

        for (const cloud of this.clouds) {
            const localMatrices =
                cloud.geometryMatrices;

            if (!localMatrices) continue;

            const worldMatrices = [];

            for (const localMatrix of localMatrices) {
                const matrix = localMatrix.clone();

                // Translate the local formation into
                // its current world position.
                matrix.m[12] += cloud.position.x;
                matrix.m[13] += cloud.position.y;
                matrix.m[14] += cloud.position.z;

                worldMatrices.push(matrix);
            }

            this.renderer.addCloud(
                worldMatrices,
                cloud.moistureRatio,
                cloud.speciesName,
                cloud.position
            );
        }

        this.renderer.update();

        this.renderDirty = false;
    }

    // ------------------------------------------
    // DIAGNOSTICS
    // ------------------------------------------

    getStats() {
        const speciesCounts = {
            cumulus: 0,
            towering: 0,
            cumulonimbus: 0
        };

        for (const cloud of this.clouds) {
            speciesCounts[cloud.speciesName]++;
        }

        return {
            totalClouds: this.clouds.length,
            speciesCounts,
            weatherRegions: this.weatherCells.size,
            rainingClouds: this.clouds.filter(
                cloud => cloud.isRaining
            ).length,
            averageWindSpeed:
                this.clouds.length
                    ? this.clouds.reduce(
                        (sum, cloud) =>
                            sum + cloud.velocity.length(),
                        0
                    ) / this.clouds.length
                    : 0
        };
    }

    dispose() {
        if (this.disposed) return;

        this.disposed = true;

        this.rainSystem.stop();

        this.renderer.mesh.dispose();

        this.clouds.length = 0;

        this.weatherCells.clear();
    }
}

export { CloudSystem };

```

src/atmosphere/cloud-generator.js
_______
```

class CloudGenerator {
    constructor(config) {
        this.config = config;
    }

    createRandom(seed = 91827) {
        let state = seed >>> 0;

        return () => {
            state = (
                Math.imul(state, 1664525) +
                1013904223
            ) >>> 0;

            return state / 4294967296;
        };
    }

    generate(
        position,
        scale,
        maxDepth = 2,
        seed = 91827
    ) {
        const matrices = [];
        const random = this.createRandom(seed);

        this.generateRecursive(
            position.clone(),
            scale,
            maxDepth,
            0,
            random,
            matrices
        );

        return matrices;
    }

    generateRecursive(
        position,
        scale,
        maxDepth,
        currentDepth,
        random,
        matrices
    ) {
        const config = this.config;
        const irregularity = config.irregularity;

        const dimensions = new BABYLON.Vector3(
            scale * config.width *
                (0.80 + random() * irregularity),
            scale * config.height *
                (0.80 + random() * irregularity),
            scale * config.depth *
                (0.80 + random() * irregularity)
        );

        const rotation =
            BABYLON.Quaternion.RotationYawPitchRoll(
                random() * 0.35,
                (random() - 0.5) * 0.22,
                (random() - 0.5) * 0.22
            );

        matrices.push(
            BABYLON.Matrix.Compose(
                dimensions,
                rotation,
                position
            )
        );

        if (currentDepth >= maxDepth) {
            return;
        }

        for (
            let i = 0;
            i < config.branchCount;
            i++
        ) {
            const angle = random() * Math.PI * 2;

            const horizontal =
                scale *
                config.horizontalSpread *
                (0.45 + random() * 0.75);

            const vertical =
                scale * config.verticalSpread;

            let verticalOffset =
                (random() * 2 - 1) * vertical;

            if (config.height > 2) {
                verticalOffset =
                    verticalOffset * 0.45 +
                    Math.abs(verticalOffset) * 0.75;
            }

            const offset = new BABYLON.Vector3(
                Math.cos(angle) * horizontal,
                verticalOffset,
                Math.sin(angle) * horizontal
            );

            const childPosition =
                position.add(offset);

            const childScale =
                scale *
                config.scaleDecay *
                (0.78 + random() * 0.38);

            this.generateRecursive(
                childPosition,
                childScale,
                maxDepth,
                currentDepth + 1,
                random,
                matrices
            );
        }
    }
}

export { CloudGenerator };

```

src/atmosphere/cloud.js

```
export class Cloud {
    constructor(position, speciesName, scale, config) {
        this.position = position.clone();
        this.speciesName = speciesName;
        this.scale = scale;
        this.baseScale = scale;
        this.config = config;

        this.waterContent = 0;
        this.maxWaterCapacity = config.moistureCapacity ?? 100;
        this.absorptionRate = config.absorptionRate ?? 16;

        this.rainIntensity = 0;
        this.rainRemaining = 0;
        this.rainDuration = config.rainDuration ?? 1.5;
        this.isRaining = false;

        // Atmospheric wind state.
        this.velocity = BABYLON.Vector3.Zero();
        this.windTarget = BABYLON.Vector3.Zero();
        this.windResponse = 0.12;

        this.localMoisture = 0;
        this.evaporationPotential = 0;
        this.environmentSampleTimer = 0;

        // Persistent geometry cache.
        this.moistureAtLastRender = -1;
        this.baseGeometryMatrices = null;
        this.baseGeometryPosition = null;

        this.geometrySeed = 1;
    }

    get moistureRatio() {
        return BABYLON.Scalar.Clamp(
            this.waterContent / this.maxWaterCapacity,
            0,
            1
        );
    }

    get isStormCloud() {
        return this.speciesName === "cumulonimbus";
    }

    updateWind(target, deltaSeconds) {
        this.windTarget.copyFrom(target);

        const response = 1 - Math.exp(
            -this.windResponse * deltaSeconds
        );

        BABYLON.Vector3.LerpToRef(
            this.velocity,
            this.windTarget,
            response,
            this.velocity
        );

        this.position.addInPlace(
            this.velocity.scale(deltaSeconds*0.01)
        );
    }
}
```

src/atmosphere/air-pressure-field.js
_______
```

class AirPressureField {
    constructor(options = {}) {
        this.time = 0;

        this.spatialScale = options.spatialScale ?? 100000;

        this.gradientStep = options.gradientStep ?? 5000;

        this.windStrength = options.windStrength ?? 0.85;

        this.maxWindSpeed = options.maxWindSpeed ?? 1.1;

        this.prevailingWind = new BABYLON.Vector3(
            0.28,
            0,
            0.12
        );

        this.wind = new BABYLON.Vector3();
    }

    update(deltaTime) {
        this.time += Math.max(0, deltaTime);
    }

    pressureAt(x, z, time = this.time) {
        const scale = this.spatialScale;

        const px = x / scale;
        const pz = z / scale;

        const wave1 = Math.sin(
            px * 0.85 +
            pz * 0.38 +
            time * 0.018
        );

        const wave2 = Math.cos(
            pz * 0.72 -
            px * 0.31 -
            time * 0.012
        );

        const wave3 = Math.sin(
            (px + pz) * 0.42 +
            time * 0.009
        );

        return (
            wave1 * 0.42 +
            wave2 * 0.34 +
            wave3 * 0.24
        );
    }

    sample(position, time = this.time) {
        const x = position.x;
        const z = position.z;
        const h = this.gradientStep;

        const pressure = this.pressureAt(
            x,
            z,
            time
        );

        // Estimate the horizontal pressure gradient.
        const gradientX = (
            this.pressureAt(x + h, z, time) -
            this.pressureAt(x - h, z, time)
        ) / (2 * h);

        const gradientZ = (
            this.pressureAt(x, z + h, time) -
            this.pressureAt(x, z - h, time)
        ) / (2 * h);

        // Air flows approximately from higher pressure
        // toward lower pressure.
        let windX =
            -gradientX *
            this.spatialScale *
            this.windStrength;

        let windZ =
            -gradientZ *
            this.spatialScale *
            this.windStrength;

        windX += this.prevailingWind.x;
        windZ += this.prevailingWind.z;

        const magnitude = Math.hypot(
            windX,
            windZ
        );

        if (magnitude > this.maxWindSpeed) {
            const factor =
                this.maxWindSpeed / magnitude;

            windX *= factor;
            windZ *= factor;
        }

        this.wind.set(
            windX,
            0,
            windZ
        );

        return {
            pressure,
            wind: this.wind.clone()
        };
    }

    getStats(position) {
        const sample = this.sample(position);

        return {
            pressure: sample.pressure,
            windSpeed: sample.wind.length(),
            windDirection: sample.wind.clone().normalize()
        };
    }
}

export { AirPressureField };
```

src/editor/building-placement.js
_______
```

class BuildingPlacement {
    constructor(system, editorCamera) {
        this.system = system;
        this.editorCamera = editorCamera;

        this.activeBuilding = null;
    }

    select(building) {
        this.activeBuilding = building;

        if (building) {
            this.editorCamera.focus(
                building.position
            );
        }
    }

    update(patch = {}) {
        const building = this.activeBuilding;

        if (!building) {
            throw new Error("No building selected.");
        }

        const position = building.position;

        if (Number.isFinite(patch.x)) {
            position.x = patch.x;
        }

        if (Number.isFinite(patch.z)) {
            position.z = patch.z;
        }

        if (Number.isFinite(patch.y)) {
            position.y = patch.y;
        }

        if (Number.isFinite(patch.rotation)) {
            building.rotation = patch.rotation;
        }

        this.system.rebuild(building);

        this.editorCamera.focus(position);
    }

    setHorizontal(x, z) {
        this.update({ x, z });
    }

    setElevation(y) {
        this.update({ y });
    }

    setRotation(rotation) {
        this.update({ rotation });
    }

    getState() {
        if (!this.activeBuilding) return null;

        return {
            position: { ...this.activeBuilding.position },
            rotation: this.activeBuilding.rotation
        };
    }
}

export { BuildingPlacement };

```

src/editor/editor-camera.js
_______
```

class EditorCamera {
    constructor(scene, spatialTransform, options = {}) {
        this.scene = scene;
        this.transform = spatialTransform;

        this.camera = new BABYLON.ArcRotateCamera(
            "BuildingEditorCamera",
            -Math.PI / 2,
            Math.PI / 3,
            options.radius ?? 80,
            BABYLON.Vector3.Zero(),
            scene
        );

        this.camera.minZ = 0.01;
        this.camera.maxZ = 1e9;

        // Permit extremely close and distant inspection.
        this.camera.lowerRadiusLimit = 0.1;
        this.camera.upperRadiusLimit = Number.MAX_VALUE;

        this.camera.wheelPrecision = 5;
        this.camera.panningSensibility = 40;
        this.camera.angularSensibilityX = 1000;
        this.camera.angularSensibilityY = 1000;

        this.camera.inertia = 0.8;
        this.camera.detachControl();
    }

    focus(position, radius) {
        const rendered = this.transform.worldToRender(
            position
        );

        this.camera.setTarget(
            new BABYLON.Vector3(
                rendered.x,
                rendered.y,
                rendered.z
            )
        );

        if (Number.isFinite(radius)) {
            this.camera.radius = Math.max(0.1, radius);
        }
    }

    attach(canvas) {
        this.camera.attachControl(canvas, true);
    }

    detach() {
        this.camera.detachControl();
    }

    dispose() {
        this.detach();
        this.camera.dispose();
    }
}

export { EditorCamera };
```

src/editor/floor-plan-height-control.js

```
/**
 * FloorPlanHeightControl
 *
 * Controls architectural wall height and thickness.
 * FloorPlanBuilder remains the authoritative owner.
 */
class FloorPlanHeightControl {
    constructor(container, builder, onCommit = null) {
        if (!container || !builder) {
            throw new Error(
                "Wall controls require a container and builder."
            );
        }

        this.container = container;
        this.builder = builder;
        this.onCommit = onCommit;

        this.element = document.createElement("section");

        Object.assign(this.element.style, {
            display: "grid",
            gap: "9px",
            margin: "12px 0",
            padding: "12px",
            background: "rgba(255,255,255,0.07)",
            borderRadius: "6px"
        });

        this.element.innerHTML = `
            <strong>ARCHITECTURAL WALLS</strong>

            <label>
                Wall height
                <output data-value="height"></output>
                <input data-setting="height"
                    type="range"
                    min="2.4"
                    max="1000"
                    step="0.1">
                <input data-number="height"
                    type="number"
                    min="2.4"
                    max="1000"
                    step="0.1">
            </label>

            <label>
                Wall thickness
                <output data-value="thickness"></output>
                <input data-setting="thickness"
                    type="range"
                    min="0.1"
                    max="1000"
                    step="0.1">
                <input data-number="thickness"
                    type="number"
                    min="0.1"
                    max="1000"
                    step="0.1">
            </label>

            <small>
                Thickness controls the horizontal depth
                of generated walls.
            </small>
        `;

        const buildButton =
            container.querySelector("#build-floor");

        if (buildButton) {
            buildButton.after(this.element);
        } else {
            container.appendChild(this.element);
        }

        this.controls = {
            height: {
                slider: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-setting="height"]'
                ),
                number: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-number="height"]'
                ),
                output: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-value="height"]'
                ),
                setter: value =>
                    builder.setWallHeight(value),
                initial: builder.wallHeight
            },
            thickness: {
                slider: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-setting="thickness"]'
                ),
                number: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-number="thickness"]'
                ),
                output: this.element.querySelector(
                    '[data-value="thickness"]'
                ),
                setter: value =>
                    builder.setWallThickness(value),
                initial: builder.wallThickness
            }
        };

        for (const [name, control] of Object.entries(
            this.controls
        )) {
            control.slider.value = control.initial;
            control.number.value = control.initial;

            control.slider.addEventListener(
                "input",
                () => {
                    control.number.value =
                        control.slider.value;

                    this.updateLabel(name);
                }
            );

            control.number.addEventListener(
                "change",
                () => {
                    control.slider.value =
                        control.number.value;

                    this.commit(name);
                }
            );

            control.slider.addEventListener(
                "change",
                () => this.commit(name)
            );

            this.updateLabel(name);
        }
    }

    updateLabel(name) {
        const control = this.controls[name];

        control.output.textContent =
            `${Number(control.slider.value).toFixed(1)} units`;
    }

    commit(name) {
        const control = this.controls[name];

        try {
            const value = Number(control.number.value);

            if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {
                throw new Error("Invalid wall dimension.");
            }

            control.setter(value);

            control.slider.value = value;
            control.number.value = value;

            this.updateLabel(name);
            this.onCommit?.(name, value);

        } catch (error) {
            console.error(
                "[FloorPlanHeightControl]",
                error
            );

            control.slider.value =
                String(control.initial = (
                    name === "height"
                        ? this.builder.wallHeight
                        : this.builder.wallThickness
                ));

            control.number.value = control.slider.value;

            this.updateLabel(name);
        }
    }

    dispose() {
        this.element.remove();
    }
}

export { FloorPlanHeightControl };

```
